# Tasks: Phase 1 — Identity and teacher onboarding

**Input**: Design documents from `/specs/002-identity/`

**Prerequisites**: [plan.md](./plan.md), [spec.md](./spec.md), [research.md](./research.md), [data-model.md](./data-model.md), [contracts/](./contracts/), [quickstart.md](./quickstart.md)

**Tests**: Included, and not optional: the working method of Phase 0's final week continues (PLAN.md, "How a phase is run"): tests before implementation, each observed red for the right reason; every new gate proven by a fixture that fails it; every job with its double-run assertion (R-API-14); every procedure under the coverage floor the phase widens (FR-213).

**Organization**: Setup, then the foundational work no story can start without (the ERD pass, the document pass, the kernel changes, the two ports, the two apps' foundations), then the five person-stories of spec.md in priority order, then the proofs and the owner's device runs. The batches at the end are the unit of delivery: one branch, one pull request, one review by Saif's review session, at most six tasks (PLAN.md).

## Format: `[ID] [P?] [Story] Description`

- **[P]**: Can run in parallel — different files, no dependency on incomplete work
- **[Story]**: US1–US5, mapping to spec.md; `[ERD]`, `[KERNEL]`, `[MOBILE]`, `[ADMIN]`, `[PROOF]` for the cross-story groups
- Every task names its file path

## Path Conventions

Three applications from one monorepo (plan.md, "Source Code"): `apps/mobile/src/` (Expo Router under `app/`, the trees under `features/`, the design under `design/`), `apps/admin/src/` (Vite, `pages/`), `apps/api/src/` (`modules/<module>/`, `platform/`, `surfaces/`, `jobs/`), `packages/`, `prisma/`, `scripts/`, `tests/`. Task branches are cut from `develop` and named `Ejazah_app/002-identity/<task id or range>`, e.g. `Ejazah_app/002-identity/T001-T006`; each opens a pull request to `develop`. A task's note goes under its line as Phase 0's did.

## Phase 1: Setup

**Purpose**: What every later task is written against.

- [x] T001 Declare the phase's configuration in `apps/api/src/platform/config/env.schema.ts` and `.env.example` together (`config.spec.ts` keeps them equal): `SUPPORT_EMAIL` (required, a valid address, no default; FR-145), `SIGN_IN_PROVIDER` (`z.enum(['fake'])`, default `fake`, refused outside development and test as every stand-in), `EMAIL_PROVIDER` (the same), `EMAIL_FROM` (an address, default a local one), the optional platform key `TRUSTED_PROXY` (the one proxy header whose last hop — the one the single trusted proxy appended, never the first — is the client address; set only with exactly one proxy in front; absent before launch, so the socket's peer is used; kernel.md), and the optional provider keys `GOOGLE_CLIENT_IDS`, `APPLE_CLIENT_ID`, `APPLE_TEAM_ID`, `APPLE_KEY_ID`, `APPLE_PRIVATE_KEY`, each classified in `envKeyKinds` (platform or provider) so the test setup strips the provider ones (contracts/ports.md) — done 2026-10-18
  > **Note under T001 (batch B1, 2026-10-18).** The ten keys, each classified in `envKeyKinds`: `SUPPORT_EMAIL`, `SIGN_IN_PROVIDER`, `EMAIL_PROVIDER`, `EMAIL_FROM` and `TRUSTED_PROXY` platform; the five provider keys provider, so `tests/support/no-provider-keys.setup.ts` strips them (the classification case asserts it). `.env.example` carries `SUPPORT_EMAIL=support@ijazah.invalid`: the key is required with no default, so a copied example must start, and the reserved domain (RFC 2606) cannot be mailed; `EMAIL_FROM` defaults to `no-reply@ijazah.invalid` the same way. `TRUSTED_PROXY` is validated as one header name and nothing else, read by its last hop (kernel.md; T017 reads it). `GOOGLE_CLIENT_IDS` parses to a non-empty list. The two selectors join `standInProviderKeys`, so the existing refusal outside development and test covers them with the same message. ## Phase 2: Foundational — the ERD pass, the document pass, the kernel, the ports, the two apps' foundations

**Purpose**: Infrastructure without which no story can start. The ERD pass comes first (R-PROC-2: before any identity code).

**⚠️ CRITICAL**: No story work begins until T008–T041 are merged.

  > **Note under T024 (batch B5, 2026-10-19).** Multipart needs a parser and the API had none: `busboy` 1.6.0, already in the tree under Nest's multer, is a pinned direct dependency of the API with the members used declared in `src/types/busboy.d.ts`; `platform/http/multipart.ts` hands on the first file unread with the fields before it, so the `kind` precedes the file (public-pages.md says so now). The helper `surfaces/upload-route.ts` (beside the surfaces, since it reads their session types, as the public-configuration router does) takes a request through the method (405 with `Allow`), the user bearer (401), the standings the route refuses (`ACCOUNT_RESTRICTED` with its detail), the body's first file, the route's `kind`, and T020's guard, then the handler, answering 201 JSON; a refusal leaves in the one shape and closes the socket once flushed, since a refused body is not read to its end. `createApplication` takes `routes` for the two handlers; `main.ts` builds them from the modules' entries. The documents route refuses `banned` only (an applicant under a request still uploads), the photos route `suspended` and `banned` as the profile editor does. A document's key is `documents/<id>.pdf` with the id minted by `randomUUID` (the row's default is v7, but the key is encrypted with the row id as associated data before the insert, so the id must exist first); the object is stored before the row and removed when the row cannot be written. The row's `document_type` is what the upload knows, `credential` or `other`: the identity kind (`national_id`, `passport`) is declared with the submission that points at the document and recorded on the pointing row (watch line for T053 and T055). A photo's key is `photos/<user id>/<uuid>.<jpg|png>`, `users.photo_key` is written through `users.repository.ts`, the replaced photo is removed from storage, and the link lives `document_link_ttl_seconds` (`platform/settings/keys.ts`, shared by both modules). `documentLinkFor` answers `NOT_FOUND` for no document and `DOCUMENT_NOT_OWNED` for another person's, minting nothing. A spec outside the port's folder may name no adapter, so the module specs run over a storage stub in `surfaces/upload-fixtures.ts` whose links expire like the real ones. Round 1 of PR #80's review: the request is piped through `stream.pipeline`, so a client that goes away mid-body destroys the parser and the file stream and the route is released with a logged line, holding nothing (finding 1, with a raw-socket case); a declared length over `upload_max_bytes` is refused before a byte is read (finding 3); a path deeper than the mount is 404 (finding 7); `replacePhotoKey` is a conditional write the database decides (finding 4). Round 2 (finding A): only the client's departure destroys the parser (`request.once('close')` while the body has not ended), never `stream.pipeline`, which would destroy the request on a parser error too and read a malformed body from a present client as an abort; that body is answered 400 and the connection closed, with a raw-socket case; the departure is watched with `stream.finished`, which calls back at once for a request already closed while the session was being resolved (finding B, with its case), where a `'close'` listener added afterwards never fires. Left as the review's suggestions: a cap on `replacePhotoKey`'s spins (a count would be a literal in a module; each spin needs another concurrent write) and the second read of `upload_max_bytes` per request. The review proposed (finding 9) a sweep of `verification_documents` that nothing points at after a window, with their objects, and a per-user cap, before B11's application flows; the owner declined it, then accepted it the same day (2026-10-19): T093, in B10, before B11's application flows.

The guarded `@formatjs/intl-pluralrules` load lives in `lib/plural-rules.ts` (R-MOBILE-9 refuses `Intl` outside `lib/`) and runs before the first message compiles; its two cases are in `lib/plural-rules.spec.ts`. Digits are the Western ones the phone boards draw (1,007 runs beside Arabic, none Arabic-Indic outside the canvas titles) and the calendar is Gregorian: `formattingTag` gives `<locale>-u-ca-gregory-nu-latn` to ICU's `#` and to `lib/formatters.ts`. Six English values carried `zero {…}`, a category CLDR English does not have, so the drawn English zero never rendered: they now read `=0 {…}`; the Arabic `zero` is CLDR's and stays. The first locale is the one the native layout direction holds (right-to-left is Arabic): the operating system sets it from the device's language on a first launch, and afterwards it holds what `syncNativeDirection` last forced, so a language choice survives the reload a change of direction needs with no store of its own. `common.a11y.filters` and `filtersActive` join both catalogues for the SearchRow primitive's trigger (the discovery keys are student copy, which `design/` must not read).

## Phase 3: User Story 1 — A person signs in once and becomes exactly one account (Priority: P1) 🎯 MVP

**Goal**: The three sign-in paths, onboarding with the single transaction, sessions with rotation and revocation, password reset, the landing by standing, the language and time zone on the account tab.

**Independent Test**: quickstart §2 — each path to the home; an abandoned flow leaves zero rows; a returning person lands with no question asked twice.

### Tests for User Story 1 ⚠️

- [x] T042 [P] [US1] `apps/api/src/modules/identity/sign-in.service.spec.ts`: a provider token verified through the stand-in with a nonce the server minted (`auth.providerNonce`), consumed once, a reused or unknown nonce refused; an unverified email treated as absent; a new identity yields an onboarding token, an existing one a session and its landing; `EMAIL_IN_USE_BY_OTHER_METHOD` with the method in all three directions (FR-120 to FR-123) — done 2026-10-20 (batch B9)
  > **Note under T042 (batch B9, 2026-10-20).** The four specs run over the real database (stage 2 carries a migrated Postgres), a clock of their own, the seeded settings through a fake reader, in-memory single-use marks, and two port fakes in `identity/__fixtures__/identity.ts`: a module may name no adapter (R-API-4), so the fakes read the sign-in stand-in's documented token shape and keep every mail, as the upload specs do (T024); the rows a spec writes or reads go through `__fixtures__/rows.repository.ts`, since only an identity repository may reach the identity tables (R-API-6). They were committed red before T047 and are green with it. A refused nonce (unknown, lapsed, used) and a refused provider token answer UNAUTHENTICATED, one answer; the nonce is taken before the token is read, so it is spent even when the token is then refused. "All three directions" of FR-123 are a Google sign-in over an Apple account, an Apple one over a Google account, and a Google one over an email account; the email sign-up over a provider account is T045's.

  > **Note under T043 (batch B9, 2026-10-20).** "A sweep over the schema" is `rowsNaming`: every text, citext, varchar and uuid column of every table of the schema, searched for the person's subject and email, after an abandoned provider flow and after a completion that fails mid-way. An email sign-up writes its code's row before any account exists (R-104); that row names the address and leaves with the purge sweep (T025), so the email flow is not part of the zero-row sweep. A completion whose transaction fails releases the `jti` claim, so the same token can try again; a used token is refused before its answers are read. The refusals of the answers are VALIDATION_FAILED with one field: `termsVersion` (not current), `timezone` (unknown, `assertTimeZone` added to `@ijazah/time`), `password` (required for an email identity, not allowed otherwise), `email` (required without a verified one, not allowed with one), `onboardingToken` (a code asked for an identity whose email is verified).

Every refusal is UNAUTHENTICATED with the default message and no details (FR-135). A teacher's standing is read from the profile once the application exists; a teacher with no profile, or one whose application status is null, resolves with no `teacher` (watch line for B11's `admits`). Round 1 of the review (observation 9): R-101's "or after a later rotation" cannot be met by the one retired-hash column; the owner chose real detection (2026-10-20), T094 in B9a, before `auth.refresh` is mounted. **Goal**: The application with its documents, the status screen in every state, resubmission and reapplication, credentials after approval.

**Independent Test**: quickstart §3's device side — apply with two subjects; see each state as the admin acts (the admin's side is US3; until it lands, the service specs drive the states).

Two contract gaps met there and settled by the owner: no column held a credential PDF's name or size, so `verification_documents.size_bytes` (nullable, migration `20261021000000_verification_documents_size_bytes`, ERD v1.7) is filled by the documents upload route from the bytes it accepted and `teacher.credentials.list` answers `document: { sizeBytes? }`; the name is never stored (it may carry the person's name), the phone shows «إجازة {subject}.pdf». And no column held the note's instant, so `noteAt` left `teacher.application.get`: `decidedAt` (`application_decided_at`, the reviewer's latest action) dates it, answered with `note` for `needs_document` and `rejected`, and for `approved`; T062 writes it on `requestInfo` as on the other decisions. The qualification is 1 to 500 characters (`QUALIFICATION_MAX_LENGTH`, no bound was stated). `submit` carries the kernel's idempotency key (`idempotent-procedures.ts` lists it) and runs inside the keyed transaction; `reapply` is not keyed. The services read and write through `stores.ts`: identity's `readApplication`/`setApplication` (`identity.service.ts` over `application.repository.ts`), catalog's `listCredentials`, `addCredential`, `replaceCredentialDocument`, `grantSubjects` (unused until the approval, B13) and `activeSubjectsAmong`, and this module's documents; every write is conditional on the state it moves from, so a request that lost a race is `CONFLICT` and leaves nothing. The first application creates the profile (`bio` empty until the profile editor, Phase 2); a reapplication clears the earlier decision, note, call record and approved screen (the audit log keeps them; review of B11, round 1) and sets `verification_status` back to `pending`. ## Phase 5: User Story 3 — An admin is a different kind of principal, and leaves a record (Priority: P1)

**Goal**: Admin sign-in with the second factor, lockout and recovery; the applications queue and its decisions; the credential review and corrections; documents and identity demands; a teacher's standing; the admins page; the audit log and the home.

**Independent Test**: quickstart §3 (the admin side) and §4.

### Tests for User Story 3 ⚠️

- [x] T060 [P] [US3] `apps/api/src/modules/admin/admin-auth.service.spec.ts`: TOTP against RFC 6238's vectors; sign-in's one answer for an unknown email, a wrong password, a disabled account, an unset password and a locked account, with the decoy verify so the timings agree within tolerance; the lockout pair against the two settings; a wrong code counts; recovery codes single-use and regenerable; setup confirms and enrolls; `resetMfa` clears, rotates, deletes the codes and revokes the sessions; the invite link single use by `password_set_at`; the throttle before the lockout (FR-170 to FR-174, SC-109) — done 2026-10-21 (batch B12a)
  > **Note under T060 (batch B12a, 2026-10-21).** RFC 6238's vectors are `platform/crypto/totp.spec.ts`'s, beside the one file that imports `otplib` (13.5.0, pinned exactly, the owner's word at the conflict check of B12): its eight-digit codes are compared by their last six digits, which is the same truncation. The decoy is proven by the work, not the clock alone: a stand-in hasher records one verify on each of the five refusals, each waiting the stand-in's delay, and the five timings agree within one verify's time, wide enough for a database the suite shares. **The throttle before the lockout** is the procedure kind's middleware, which no service sees: its case is T067's router spec (B12b). The invite link's cases (single use, expiry from settings, any part altered, another key, a disabled account, a breached password leaving the link usable) are here; `admins.service.spec.ts`, `admin-invite.spec.ts` and `jobs/deliver-admin-invite.spec.ts` hold T066's own. A wrong second-factor or recovery code answers `CODE_INVALID`; a token that is no session waiting for its second factor, or an account locked meanwhile, answers `ADMIN_SIGN_IN_FAILED`, so the panel starts again; `MFA_SETUP_REQUIRED` answers `mfa.verify` and `mfa.recover` for an administrator not enrolled; `setup.*` on an enrolled one is `FORBIDDEN`.

## Phase 6: User Story 4 — An approved teacher sets up payout and stays invisible until the gateway says so (Priority: P2)

**Goal**: Payout registration through the port, the recipient events routed by hash, the four payout screens, the standing that refuses publishing.

**Independent Test**: quickstart §6.

### Tests for User Story 4 ⚠️

- [x] T075 [P] [US4] `apps/api/src/modules/identity/payout.service.spec.ts` and `apps/api/src/modules/payments/recipient-events.spec.ts`: `register` stores the reference encrypted and row-bound with its hash and `payout_configured_at`, clears `payout_verified_at` (first setup and replacement); a signed `recipient_verified` event sets the instant, a `recipient_rejected` the reason; a replayed event id changes nothing; an unknown hash is recorded, reported and changes no row; no raw account number in any column, log line or response (a sweep over the stand-in's records) (FR-190 to FR-194)
- [x] T076 [P] [US4] `apps/api/src/surfaces/procedures.spec.ts` gains "`admits: 'publishable'` refuses an approved teacher whose payout is unverified and admits one whose payout is verified" (FR-192)

### Implementation for User Story 4

- [x] T077 [US4] `apps/api/src/modules/identity/payout.service.ts` (the port's `registerPayoutRecipient`, the reference encrypted with its lookup hash on identity's own table, `markPayoutVerified(hash)` / `markPayoutRejected(hash, reason)` exported from `identity/index.ts`), and the webhook branch in `payments/webhook.controller.ts` with `payments/recipient-events.service.ts`, which records the signed event and calls identity's two entries (payments → identity, never back; research R-121), `PayoutDetails` published as provisional in `packages/contracts/src/payout.ts`; `teacher.payout.get` and `register` in `identity/payout.router.ts` (`admits: 'approved'`); the refusal cases. After T075, T076

  > **Note under T077 (the conflict check of B15, 2026-10-26).** The owner decided six points on the reviewer's advice. **(1)** `payments/webhook.controller.ts` did not exist; only the signature guard did. B15 builds `POST /webhooks/payment`, which captures the raw body with its own size limit, verifies it through the guard's core, and records the event under its unique `provider_event_id` before any action (R-MONEY-8). This phase acts on recipient events only. An order event, which cannot arrive while no order exists, is reported and answered 2xx with no row written, since rule 20 refuses one without a payment. **(2)** `recipient_rejected` carries the gateway's `reason` where it sent one. This is an additive change to the port and the stand-in. The reason is kept as plain text, cut to `PAYOUT_REJECTED_REASON_MAX_LENGTH`; without one, `PayoutRejected` shows a generic line. **(3)** `scripts/dev/emit-recipient-event.ts` signs its own body under the stand-in's derived secret, and reads and decrypts the teacher's reference. It refuses to run unless `NODE_ENV` is development or test and both the database and the API are local. Its event id derives from the reference, the verdict and the reason, so repeating a command is a replay. **(4)** T076 gains the replacement case (FR-193): a verified teacher who registers again is refused by `publishable`. **(5)** `register` calls the payment port outside any database transaction, before the idempotency key's transaction opens. A retry may register a second recipient at the gateway before being answered from the stored row; Phase 2's hosted onboarding revisits this. **(6)** **For Phase 2:** no task of this phase reads `PaymentPort.getRecipientStatus`, the poll fallback of the payout gate (FR-PAYG-15), so Phase 2's payout or gateway task adds it. T078's third entry point, the banner of `PackagesLocked`, is the one T034 left for this batch (its header comment), beside the two T078 names. B15 also ran as three parallel lanes (identity, payments with the script, the phone) after a step zero that fixed the contract and the shapes between them; this was the owner's decision of 2026-10-26.
- [x] T078 [US4] The payout screens in `apps/mobile/src/features/teacher/payout/`: `PayoutStart` (the button calls `register` with the provisional details on a development build; the hosted onboarding is Phase 2's), `PayoutSetup`, `PayoutVerified`, `PayoutRejected` (the gateway's reason, the support line from the cached `supportEmail`); routes under `app/(teacher)/payout/`; the `ApplicationApproved` row 1 and the `TeacherAccount` payout row land here; render tests (FR-190 to FR-194). After T034, T077
- [x] T079 [P] [US4] `scripts/dev/emit-recipient-event.ts`: drives the stand-in's signed webhook with a verified or rejected verdict for a teacher (quickstart §6), reusing the stand-in's derived secret; a spec that a replay is a no-op end to end

**Checkpoint**: An approved teacher registers, is rejected with a reason, is verified, and is refused by a publishable kind in between.

## Phase 7: User Story 5 — A person deletes their account, in the app or on the web (Priority: P2)

**Goal**: The in-app deletion with its three steps and the one transaction, the web deletion, the confirmation mail, the public routes with their headers.

**Independent Test**: quickstart §5.

### Tests for User Story 5 ⚠️

- [x] T080 [P] [US5] `apps/api/src/modules/identity/deletion.service.spec.ts`: the typed word in either language; re-authentication by a fresh provider token (nonce) whose subject is one of the caller's own `auth_identities` rows, or the password against the caller's own hash; a valid token of another identity refused; the one transaction erasing every personal field of ADR 0015 D3, deleting `auth_identities`, revoking every session (`account_deleted`), removing push tokens, erasing a teacher's bio, documents and payout columns, keeping the audit rows; the Apple revoke through the port with the stored refresh token; the email freed (a new sign-up with it succeeds); the consequences registry called with `account_deleted`; the confirmation mail after commit (FR-140 to FR-142, SC-111)

  > **Note under T080 (the conflict check of B16, 2026-10-26).** The owner decided these points on the reviewer's advice. **(1)** `verification_documents` belongs to the verification module, credentials and profiles point at its rows, and its key is NOT NULL. An additive migration makes the key nullable and adds `erased_at`. The verification module subscribes `account_deleted` in the consequences registry and sets only `erased_at` in the deletion's transaction; the key stays. After the commit each file is removed through the storage port, and its key is cleared only once the removal succeeds. A worker job retries every row that has `erased_at` and still has a key, so an identity document never outlives its key unseen (Egypt PDPL). The profile photo follows the same rule: the key goes only after its file is gone. **(2)** One consequences registry is built in `main.ts`, shared by the admin module's standing actions and this deletion; `TerminationCause` gains `account_deleted`. **(3)** The Apple revoke runs after the commit, outside the transaction; a failure is reported and the deletion stands. **(4)** The web copy that names sessions, payments or the ijazah verification page, none of which exists in this phase, is replaced by `web.deleteAccount.honest.*`; the board's lines stay in the catalogue (R-HONEST, as in B14d-2 and B15). **(5)** See the note under T082. **(6)** `account.delete` takes the idempotency key: app-surface.md's guarantees require it, and its table row had left it out. **(7)** The API renders two groups of the phone catalogue, `web.deleteAccount` and `web.notFound`, copied under `check:generated`; `web.verify` is Phase 4's. The batch ran as three parallel lanes (the API, the web pages, the phone) after a step zero.

After T052, T082

- [x] T097 [US5] A banned person's deletion keeps their sign-in identifiers, so the ban outlives the account (FR-155; B16 review, round 1, question 1; the owner's decision of 2026-10-26). A new identity table, `banned_identifiers`, holds keyed hashes only: HMAC-SHA256 under a subkey derived from `ENCRYPTION_KEY` (HKDF, a label of its own), of the normalized email and of each linked `(provider, provider_subject)`, with `created_at`, and nothing else of the person. Rows are written in the deletion's transaction when `users.account_status` is `banned`. They are checked after the person proved the address or the account, never before: `auth.email.verifySignUpCode` after the code, `auth.signInWithProvider` on the new-account path after the token, and `onboarding.complete` before the row is written. A match is refused with `ACCOUNT_RESTRICTED`; no token is issued and nothing says an account was deleted before. The phone shows the banned state without a session. The migration, ERD, data-model and module-tables entries; ADR 0015 D1 and D3 name the exception to the freed email. Specs: a banned person deletes and is refused at sign-up by the same email, and by the same Google or Apple subject; an ordinary person deletes and signs up again. After T082

  > **Note under T097 (its conflict check, 2026-10-26).** The owner approved six points with three conditions. **(1)** The checks run after proof of ownership, never at the code request, which answers every address alike (R-104); otherwise anyone could learn of another person's ban. **(2)** The refusal is the existing `ACCOUNT_RESTRICTED` (403) with no detail. No onboarding token, access token or refresh token is issued, and nothing says an account was deleted before. The phone shows the banned screen's title, its final line and the support contact without a session. **(3)** An HMAC cannot be recomputed without the address it was made from, so rotating `ENCRYPTION_KEY` leaves the stored identifiers unmatched. The warning goes into RUNBOOK.md's key-rotation steps themselves, not only into a follow-up. **(4)** The identifiers are kept with `created_at` and no end date. OPEN-05 (NFR-COMP-2) must settle their retention before launch, and the privacy policy must say it. **(5)** Done as B16's sixth task: a step zero, then an API lane and a phone lane. Saif's next round is round 2 for B16's fixes and round 1 for T097; a Critical or a Major in T097 gets its own round 2. **(6)** The branch is renamed `…/T080-T097`; T098 belongs to the nearest batch.

No API change: `account.delete` already admits a restricted account. Not in B16 (new behaviour with its own tests); done in the nearest batch, and before Phase 1 closes and before any store submission. After T084 — done 2026-10-27 (batch B17)

The task names no API change and makes none; one spec case is added outside its named paths, in `apps/api/src/modules/identity/account.router.spec.ts`: `account.delete` admits a **banned** account (the suspended case existed). **Purpose**: What closes the phase: the adversarial subjects, the floor, the register's re-count, the dev scripts the quickstart names, the runbook, and the two device steps that are the owner's.

  > **Note under T085 (B17, 2026-10-27).** "Disagrees in both directions" holds for `noGenderConjunct` only. `asymmetricV11` leaks to a female requester alone; a male requester sees exactly what the rule shows him, so no case in that direction can disagree, and asserting one would be false. The block asserts `asymmetricV11` caught in the female direction and finding nothing wrong in the male one. A bite in the male direction would need a mirrored third subject, which this task does not name. A control case per direction holds the reference subject to no disagreement, so a bite cannot come from a comparison that disagrees with everything.

The device: the owner's Android 16 phone, a development build from EAS Build's free plan (profile `development`, an APK, `expo-dev-client` 58.0.15), reached over wireless adb with `adb reverse` for Metro (8081) and the API (3000); `SIGN_IN_PROVIDER=fake` and the development sign-in. batching is off on both surfaces (`allowBatching: false`; both clients use `httpLink`), so a batch request is refused 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` whole; the error shape carries its status as a non-enumerable `data.httpStatus`, from the same mapping, so no body changes, and each surface response has an `'error'` listener, attached before the check, that logs one warn line with the error's code and message only and writes nothing, so a failed write that reaches tRPC's exception handler cannot end the process; specs hold each of these followed by a valid call answering 200 on the same server, every error body unchanged byte for byte, and the warn line holding nothing of the request; while an error of `createContext` or of our code stays an unexpected 500, reported; the refusal is logged once at info with its correlation id and nothing of the body, and never reported; `toErrorShape` is unchanged, so a SyntaxError of our own stays an unexpected 500; a gate spec refuses a kind without the middleware, an HTTP spec boots the application with the real routers and is red with 500 before the change, and a spec holds that neither the log nor the reporter receives any part of the body; no contract change. After T099, T150

  > **Note under T103 to T108 (B19a's conflict check, the owner's decisions of 2026-10-28).** B19 is split in two, each within six tasks: B19a fixes the defects (T103 to T108); B19b, the close (T091, T092 and the local `test:db-integrity` on a database of its own), starts only after B19a merges, so T091 walks the fixed screens and T092 closes on the final `develop`. Each defect was researched before its decision. T103: `Stack.Protected`, or a guard that keeps the navigator mounted, would change every landing and has three open SDK 58 bugs; left for a task of its own. T104: the picker's two forms are probably two mechanisms, a stalled Android push animation ([react-native-screens#4930](https://github.com/software-mansion/react-native-screens/issues/4930)) and a JavaScript thread frozen by 418 `localeCompare` calls ([facebook/hermes#867](https://github.com/facebook/hermes/issues/867)); only the device can tell, so the device decides. T105: `forceDarkOn` does nothing for apps that target Android 13 or later ([react-native-webview#3253](https://github.com/react-native-webview/react-native-webview/issues/3253)), and whether `prefers-color-scheme` reaches the page is not confirmed, so the app passes its scheme explicitly; the served `tokens.css` already carries `[data-theme="dark"]` from `tokens.json`, and nothing selected it. T108: plain tRPC 11.19.0 answers these 400 `BAD_REQUEST` with the SyntaxError as cause; our `toErrorShape` mapped by that cause and answered 500 INTERNAL, logged and reported with a message that can quote the body; no tRPC release changes this. A reader that parses the body before tRPC was not taken, since it would read every body before authentication and turn T150's anonymous-administrator 401 into 413; nor mapping `BAD_REQUEST` with a SyntaxError cause in `toErrorShape`, which cannot tell a broken input from a SyntaxError thrown by one of our own schemas, wrapped by tRPC the same way.

  > **Note under T103 to T108 (the device checks, 2026-10-28, on the owner's Android phone, at most five attempts each by the owner's rule).** T103: each case walked once, landing where the guard says, and the back from it closing the app or returning to Welcome, never to a protected screen, with no `GO_BACK` in Metro's log: a student signing out; the account deleted (T098); a teacher signing out from the application's status after a submitted application, with a one-page test PDF made for it, pushed to the phone as `Download/ejazah-test.pdf` and removed after; a teacher signing out from the suspended screen; a suspended and a banned account (their status set in the local database on a test account); a deep link to `ijazah://otp`, kept on the banned screen while signed in and, signed out, opening the code screen with Welcome behind it. The test accounts, their documents, their stored files and the two banned identifiers the deletion of the banned one left were removed from the local database afterwards (the owner, 2026-10-28). T105: the served Terms read in the dark and the light scheme, on the token background with the gutter. T106: no light flash: three rounds of the stack and modal transitions in the dark scheme, recorded as raw frames on the phone, 825 frames, the brightest frame's mean luma 46 of 255; the navigation theme stays a follow-up for the redesign. Follow-up, not in this batch (the owner, 2026-10-28): a deep link to `ijazah://otp` while signed out opens the code screen with no address («We sent a code to» and nothing after it); it should return to Welcome when no address is pending. It predates this batch.

CI sets it in stage 2's tests and stage 4's integrity probes. With it on, the URLs come from the environment only. The proof `tests/pipeline/database-spec-gate.spec.ts` counts connections to a local listener: none without the variable, a refusal naming the missing URL with it on and no URL in the environment. `specs/003-booking/quickstart.md` says so. Fixed by tagging the describe and reading the URL with `specDatabaseUrl`.

  > **Note under T145 (the owner's decision of 2026-11-02).** The library is `sharp` 0.35.5: `rotate()` with no argument first (the EXIF orientation applied to the pixels), then a re-encode in the same type (JPEG stays JPEG, PNG stays PNG), with no `withMetadata` or `keepMetadata`; `limitInputPixels` at a bound fit for a profile photo, proposed with its reason in the batch's report; a file `sharp` cannot decode is refused as an input error and nothing is stored. Specs: a JPEG carrying GPS and device data is stored with no EXIF at all; a JPEG with Orientation 6 is stored turned upright with no Orientation; a PNG's text chunks are removed; a non-image named `.jpg` is refused and nothing is written; an image larger than the bound is refused. `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` must run on the pipeline's Linux with no `onlyBuiltDependencies`, and the lockfile carries `sharp`'s optional packages for the platforms used.   > **Done (B27).** `POST /uploads/photos` reads the header with `sharp` 0.35.5, refuses a file it cannot read and one over `PHOTO_MAX_INPUT_PIXELS` (`VALIDATION_FAILED`, before any pixel is decoded and before anything is stored, the previous photo kept), then `rotate()` and a re-encode in the same type with no metadata kept: no EXIF, XMP, IPTC, ICC or PNG text chunk. The bound proposed for the owner: 8192 × 6144 = 50,331,648 pixels, the largest frame of a phone's 50 MP sensor (12 and 24 MP defaults and 48/50 MP full-resolution modes pass; 108 and 200 MP originals do not), a decode of 144 to 384 MiB at most against sharp's default of about 268 million pixels; `upload_max_bytes` alone does not bound it, since a small PNG can claim any size. The constant lives in `platform/guards/upload.middleware.ts`, outside the named path, since the magic-number gate refuses a literal under `modules/`. JPEG at sharp's default quality; `failOn: 'warning'` kept. No install script; the lockfile records the `@img/sharp-*` packages for linux-x64 and win32-x64 with no `onlyBuiltDependencies`. (2) Only a PNG over the bound is specced; a JPEG whose SOF claims more would cover the other decoder, and the decoder's own `limitInputPixels` is not held alone, since `metadata()` honours the constructor's limit. (3) `metadata()` already applies `limitInputPixels`, so a file over sharp's default of about 268 million pixels answers «cannot be read» and one between 50 and 268 million «more pixels than allowed»; one `sharp(bytes, { limitInputPixels })` for both calls would give one message, if the two need not differ.

  > **Done (B22).** Every call of `apps/mobile/src/lib/client.ts` takes a timeout through one helper over `AbortController`: 30 s for the calls, the retry and `auth.refresh` (`CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`), 120 s for the uploads (`UPLOAD_TIMEOUT_MS`, a 10 MiB file over a slow network). The timeout lasts until the answer's body is read (PR #112's review, round 1, Minor 4, the owner's decision of 2026-11-01): the body is read inside the timer and handed on as a new `Response`, so an answer that stalls after its headers fails too. - [x] T156 One refresh at a time on the phone, and a refusal of a token already replaced never signs out (found by T092's run, §3, 2026-10-29, seen four times in five attempts; the owner's decision of 2026-10-29): after the system back key closes the app, its JavaScript runtime stays alive with the old screen tree mounted, and the next launch mounts a second tree in the same runtime; each tree's `ApiProvider` makes its own client, whose refresh is single only within that client (`apps/mobile/src/lib/client.ts`, `refreshing`), so with an expired access token both refreshed the same token at once (two `refresh start` 20 ms apart), the server rotated one and refused the other (`UNAUTHENTICATED`), and that client cleared the tokens and signed the person out of a live session. The refresh becomes one per runtime (module level), and an `UNAUTHENTICATED` answer to a refresh whose token is no longer the stored one is not a sign-out; a spec; a device check in T092's run: the old steps (signed in, closed with the back key, the access token expired, opened again) keep the person signed in. Batch B19d, before T092 — done 2026-10-29 (batch B19d)

- [ ] T157 Two refreshes of the same token at once: the loser is refused (T156's server side, found by T092's run, 2026-10-29; the owner's decision of 2026-10-29: the server is left as it is, recorded with its reason): `refresh` in `apps/api/src/modules/identity/session.service.ts` finds the presented token current for both requests; the first rotates it, the second's conditional update (`apps/api/src/modules/identity/user-sessions.repository.ts`, `rotate`) matches no row and is refused with `UNAUTHENTICATED`, the session left alive. Answering the loser through the grace path would rotate from the winner's token and retire it, so a client that kept the winner's answer would be revoked for reuse at its first refresh after the grace window (`findSessionByRetiredHash`); the server cannot hand the loser the winner's token, which it holds only as a hash, and a code of its own for the race would break FR-135. A change is made only with a probe in `tests/db-integrity` that covers the whole case: two refreshes at once, the client going on with either answer, a refresh after the grace window, the session not revoked. T156 removes the phone's way into it. Not scheduled in Phase 1

## Dependencies & Execution Order

### Phase Dependencies

- **Setup (Phase 1)**: No dependencies; T001–T006 in B1 (T003 deferred to B2 with the migrations that create its tables), T007 in B3
- **Foundational (Phase 2)**: Depends on Setup; blocks every story. Within it: the ERD pass (T008–T013, the schema check in the same batch) first, in migration order; the document pass (T014–T015) beside it; the kernel (T016–T021) after T013, carrying the idempotency-actor and audit-label migrations with their writers; the ports and uploads (T022–T024); the two foundations (T030–T037) after T006/T007 and before the gates (T026–T028), because T028 couples the phone's feature tree to the module specs; the sweeps (T025) with the ports; T029 with T048, whose procedure its snapshot lookup needs, its allow-list landing with T038; T038–T040 beside them; T041 last
- **US1 (P1)**: After Foundational; the services (T042–T049) before the screens (T050–T052)
- **US2 (P1)**: After Foundational; independent of US1's services, but its screens need T034 and the teacher landing of T052
- **US3 (P1)**: After Foundational; T064's standing service is what US4's `publishable` and US5's consequences registry build on, so US3's services come before US4 and US5
- **US4 (P2)**: After US3's T068 (the standing) and US2's T057 (the approved teacher)
- **US5 (P2)**: After US1's T047 (sessions, the provider port's revoke) and US3's T068 (the registry)
- **Proofs (Phase 8)**: T085–T088 any time after Foundational; T089 after T085; T090 after the teacher flow; T091 after every screen; T092 last

### Cross-story notes

- **The one consequences entry.** `identity/consequences.ts` (T068) is the seam deletion, suspension, revocation and ban all call; it is empty in this phase and Phase 2 and Phase 3 register their enrollment and session consequences on it. Nothing in this phase cancels a session or an enrollment, because none exists.
- **The profile editor and the payout row.** `ApplicationApproved` links to three next steps; row 1 (payout) is T078's, row 2 (the public profile editor, FR-PROFILE-1) is Phase 2's and the link lands then, row 3 (`PackagesLocked`) is T034's locked state.
- **No notification row is written** (FR-154): the status, payout and credentials screens carry the state on the next open.
- **The admin invite needs the email stand-in's mail folder** to be read by the owner (quickstart §4); the first admin is the seed's.
- **Watch list from the conflict review (2026-10-18)**, one line per batch, each a gate that goes red unless the batch does what the line says:
  - B4 T017: `main.ts` boots with stand-in session resolvers (the real ones are T047 and T066).
  - B6 (the phone) and B8 (the admin): the render tests need a React Native or jsdom Vitest environment; the root config is `node`, so the environment lands with the first screen task of each app.
  - B9 T047, B11 T056/T057, B13a T069, B13b T068: `gender/require-requester-gender` (stage 3) refuses an exported repository method returning a type named `Teacher*` (`TeacherProfile`, `TeacherCredential`, and `Pick`/`Omit` of them) without a requester-gender parameter; return freshly built row types, or take the documented escape hatch with its reason.
  - B10 T049, B13b T068, B16 T082: every new non-public procedure has a refusal spec naming its path in the same batch, or the authorization gate (T028) is red.
  - B10 T051: a city list under `packages/time/src/**` needs a `packages/time/src/**/*.spec.ts` change in the same batch (the TIME suite gate).
  - B12 T065/T067: the home's queue counts read through `verification/index.ts` and `catalog/index.ts` entries added in B12 (the latter under the gate's exception of T038, which names `index.ts`).
  - B17 T086: measure coverage with the widened include before raising the floor.
  - B5 T023 (the email port): outside development and test, refuse a `SUPPORT_EMAIL` or `EMAIL_FROM` under a reserved domain (`.invalid`, `.test`, `.example`, `.localhost`, `example.com/net/org`), naming the key and never the value, as the stand-ins are refused; a copied `.env.example` would otherwise show the placeholder on four screens and send from it (PR #75 review, follow-up 3).
  - Every batch that adds a procedure or an enum member runs `pnpm contract:snapshot` in the same change (B2, B5, B10–B13, B15, B16).

### Within each story

- Tests first, observed red for the right reason; then the service, then the router with its schemas and refusal cases, then the screens from their boards
- A screen task names its board and reads `docs/design/screens.md`'s row and `components.md`'s contracts; copy from the catalogue only
- Every public procedure declares its rate limit; every procedure its output; every non-public procedure its refusal spec for the gate (T028)

### Parallel Opportunities

- Setup: T002–T007 parallel after T001
- Foundational: T008, T014, T015 parallel with T009; T016, T018–T023, T025 parallel after T013; T030, T031, T033, T035, T036, T038–T040 parallel; T026–T028, T040, T041 after the two foundations
- US1: T042–T046 parallel; T049 beside T047
- US2: T053–T055 parallel; T056 and T057 parallel
- US3: T060–T065 parallel; T066–T069 parallel after their specs; T070–T073 parallel after their routers
- US4: T075, T076, T079 parallel
- US5: T080, T081 parallel

## Batches (PLAN.md: at most six tasks, one branch, one pull request, one review)

The order is the dependency order above; a batch is cut from `develop` after the one before it merges. Two batches in flight never write the same file or mark adjacent lines of this file.

A branch is named by the first and last task id of its batch; where a batch is not contiguous (B1, B2, B3, B5, B7, B10, B12a, B12b, B13a, B13b, B14b-2) the tasks column is the list, and no batch is specs alone: every batch merges green (R-PROC-4). Eighteen batches for seven working days was the honest count, nineteen since B9a (T094, a security need the review of B9 found), twenty since B10 was split into its API and its screens (the owner's decision of 2026-10-20 at its conflict check: seven tasks, three of them phone screens, in one review), twenty-one since B11 was split the same way (2026-10-21), twenty-two since B12 was (2026-10-21), twenty-four since B13 was split in three (2026-10-22: twenty-three procedures and the phone's screens in one review), twenty-seven since B14 was split in four (2026-10-22: about seventeen pages and fifteen dialogs, each task its own review), twenty-eight since B14b was split in two (2026-10-22: T071 alone was about three thousand lines, and its viewer needs a library and API changes), twenty-nine since B18's device run found defects that moved T091 and T092 to B19 (2026-10-28), thirty since B19 was split into its defects and its close (2026-10-28), thirty-one since T091's board pass moved T092 to B19c (2026-10-28), thirty-two since T122 moved T092 to B19d (2026-10-29); PLAN.md's rule applies: an overflow is stated the day it is seen, with its cause, and goes to the descope ladder rather than being absorbed. The batches most likely to be the overflow's cause, in order, are B14 (the admin pages), B10 (the phone screens) and B4 (the kernel); the specification's Risk assumption names the same three.

Re-batched on 2026-10-18 after the conflict review that followed T003: T013 joined B2 (the schema check runs on every pull request), the two kernel migrations moved to B4 with their writers, the phone's foundation comes before the gates that couple it to the module specs, T029 landed with the procedure it maps, and T074 with the services it reads. The review's watch list is under "Cross-story notes".

**Placed 2026-11-21 (`PLAN.md`, «The calendar to the closed test»).** R1a 11/21–11/23; R1b 11/24–11/25, cut after the programme's B-P2 merges (both write `share-release.rules.ts`), with `004:` T084, its device run on the phones day, the tasks' last day (amended 2026-11-23, the owner's «موافق»: R1b merges without it); R2 11/25–11/27; R3 11/28–11/29 (its build on 12/5, with the logo and the design). The before-launch tasks outside these batches are thirteen, fourteen with T188 (added 2026-11-26, R2's Q-D1; batch 1 then holds seven) (PR #155's review, round 1, N5: `003:` T118 is «reviewed at Phase 6's load pass», not before launch), all before the closed test (the owner, 2026-11-21, «لا، كله قبل 12/6»), each confirmed in its batch's conflict check: batch 1, 11/30–12/1: T148, T149, T150, T152, T153, T154, and T188 (added 2026-11-26, the owner, R2's Q-D1; seven tasks); batch 2, 12/2–12/3: `003:` T081, T083, T086, T092, T094, and `004:` T085 (added 2026-11-22, the owner; six tasks); the Terms' text, T151 with `003:` T117 (the same files; T117's «its own small batch»), with the store's preparation on 12/2–12/3. R1a runs on B-P1's days and shares with it the migration order, the ERD's numbering, the seed's counts and the settings registry (`specs/006-programme/tasks.md`, «Batches»). R1b's `004:` T084 and R2's T177 both write `platform/config/env.schema.ts`, `config.spec.ts`, `.env.example` and `docs/RUNBOOK.md`: R2 is cut after R1b merges, and its deploy sets the new media secret (PR #155's review, round 2, N2).

## Implementation Strategy

### MVP scope

B1–B10: a person signs in by each path, onboards once, lands by standing, and manages language and time zone. Everything a later batch needs from the kernel exists after B7.

### Incremental delivery

- B11 adds the teacher's application and credentials; B12–B14 the admin who decides them — the first full journey (SC-101) is demonstrable after B14.
- B15 adds payout; B16 deletion; B17 the proofs; B18 the owner's timed run and the defects it found; B19a the defects of the screen pass; B19b both languages on the device, and the close.

### Notes

- A task's note goes under its line, as in Phase 0; a decision taken while implementing is recorded there with the owner's word where one was asked.
- Jira is a read-only archive (owner's decision, 2026-10-17): no card is created, moved or commented for any task here.
- The branch rule (`pnpm check:branch`) reads this folder's name: every batch branch is `Ejazah_app/002-identity/<ids>`.
